# Native simulation core delivery

[Feature](../projects/native-simulation-core-feature-spec.md) · [Technical design](../projects/native-simulation-core-tech-design.md) · [Limits](../limits/native-simulation-core.md)

## Current scope

**Full-game destination.** Stand-alone Rust is the chosen server. The [full-game design and implementation checklist](#full-game-on-the-stand-alone-server) extend NSC04 beyond the measured opening. The earlier proof and performance batch boundaries below describe their respective assignments. The ordinary launcher remains TypeScript until full-game cutover.

**Project authorization includes the designed implementation at a 2 GiB transient allowance.** Mike's November 13 instruction expanded the earlier NSC01–NSC03 boundary through supported-game conversion, bounded parallelism, same-machine cells, controlled distributed custody, adaptive grouping and cleanup. The current execution boundary below selects the batch to finish before review. The 64/128 MiB rejections remain historical evidence. Numerical improvement targets guide isolated candidate decisions; correctness, privacy, deterministic effects, genuine admission, atomic storage and actual completed gameplay remain required.

**Proof and RGS08 execution boundaries.** The selected NSC01–NSC03 continuation at `9a37818b484d64a581c5894e6fd5fc7f036f0640` is complete. The subsequent stand-alone server assignment ends at the [RGS08 decision](completed/2026/rust-game-server.md). Use the normal clock, one-second saves and 2 GiB Rust profile. The next mechanic, wider crowd tuning, broader conversion, cell migration and distribution were outside that review boundary.

Open Legend is an authoritative multiplayer server world. Login, reconnect and returning expose the current permitted world; ordinary background persistence belongs in gameplay costs. Cold server recovery and administrative checkpoint rewind are separate measurement windows. The combined save/rewind journey remains correctness and aggregate-cost evidence, not a steady-play result. R1–R9 retained implementations and measured experiment dispositions are common to both arms; the older 32.18% JavaScript bookkeeping attribution has a different workload/denominator and cannot explain the combined journey's 11.1% reduction.

This tracker owns native-core conversion and its experiments. [PF10/PF11](performance.md#pf10--workers-and-movement-prediction) retains worker/operating and population qualification; [D6/PD12](production-deployment.md#conditional-launch-and-expansion) retains production distributed delivery. Do not duplicate their task bodies or claim their completion from a proof. [BK29](production-data.md#bk29--pressure-and-preparation-work-removal-proposed) remains the owner of current-runtime semantic/retention improvements; take its accepted source as common behavior when comparing.

The [MMORPG foundations research](../../research/mmorpg-foundations/README.md) and [focused needs-design register](mmorpg-scalability-needs-design.md) identify additional preparation for large coordinates, sparse physical residency, independent seed-world lifecycles and cross-owner capacity contracts. Reconcile these against the actual implementation branch before proposing new work. They do not replace NSC's design, duplicate its implementation queue, change its stage order or establish capacity.

The [native-runtime-foundations continuation](native-runtime-foundations.md#current-selection-and-remaining-work) owns J/S reconsideration at 2 GiB, P's compatible accepted-cut attribution and justified sharing, and R's benefit attribution, serial extraction, R0 dispatch and R1 ordered joins. Reuse its complete reviewed recipes and existing owners. Earlier J/S code removals and measurements remain reproducible; neither an old resource failure nor a missed percentage target substitutes for completing other authorized stages. The [player-view lifecycle repairs](native-runtime-foundations.md#player-view-lifecycle-review--november-13-2026) remain in the control; fresh comparisons include them in both Native arms.

## Current progress

**Stand-alone server workload.** At `49cce3620`, TypeScript, Node-hosted Rust and stand-alone Rust each completed three ordinary-play runs with one player, ten human NPCs, ten deer, five hares and 1,246 scenery records. Each run completed 34 visible and saved walks, two steering gestures and two saved conversations. Both Rust hosts used one simulation worker. The [RGS08 comparison](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-18-rust-game-server.md#rollback-qualified-comparison) records costs and the [caller inventory](native-simulation-core-coverage.md#stand-alone-server-caller-inventory) carries the verified scope into NSC04.

**Selected NSC01–NSC03 batch complete; measured Native adoption rejected.** The [November 17 decision](#nsc03-disposition--november-17-2026) records the matched six-run comparison, scoped E2 conclusion, complete lifetime/lifecycle work, final caller repairs and resource receipt. The earlier checkpoints below retain their original source/profile/spending provenance. Another mechanic and wider conversion/distribution remain outside this review boundary.

## Current implementation batch

The development NPC AI allowance now defaults to 1,000 under [LA231](../limits/cognition.md#la231). Source and Native decisions for independent NPCs use the same configured job and provider allowances, and both real caller checks pass concurrent ordinary work, configured saturation, actor ordering and exact settlement. Native saves a fourth reply while three unrelated providers remain held. Optional maintenance now retains cancellation while acquiring its lease and reading current admission. The Native held-admission caller confirms that cancellation suppresses dispatch and that a later eligible pass saves once. The [isolated four-area admission comparison](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-17-native-active-area-comparisons.md#isolated-admission-comparison) qualifies all six runs. Complete CPU ranges overlap and reservations peak higher under the new policy without a matching increase in sampled concurrent RAM. Keep the policy for verified independence.

The original frozen active-area campaign's nine missing replies were refused at the shared immediate admission check before speech, job creation or provider dispatch. Exact disposable HTTP replays cover an occupied ordinary position and both immediate positions occupied by urgent thoughts. The original timing artifacts locate the same check but omit live occupancy. Directed Send in both runtimes now waits briefly outside the admission lanes for genuine contention under [LA231](../limits/cognition.md#la231). The final repaired callers pass continuation, exact-ID repeat, expiry, disconnect, held-reply/view-loss and fresh PostgreSQL reopening. The [requalification evidence](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-17-native-active-area-comparisons.md#conversation-admission-repair-and-requalification) preserves the separate old-policy campaigns.

The [current-policy width comparison](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-17-native-active-area-comparisons.md#current-policy-worker-width-and-active-area-scaling) qualifies all 18 runs at one, two and four independently active areas. It rejects two-range publication. Every four-area pair uses more complete CPU at width two, and neither progress nor the visible tail reaches the benefit gate. Keep inline encoding and the separately verified same-world dispatch owner. The prior-visible and dirty-source spatial lookup screens close only those lookups. The four-area visible tail remains above the small-world reference despite meeting the steady game rate. That preceding batch settled at $40.475639 cumulative of the $100 continuing ceiling, with zero holds and no paid provider usage. The resumed proof supplies the [current NSC03 decision](#nsc03-disposition--november-17-2026).

## Remaining proof checklist

The first matched standard-worker campaign completed three Source and two Native end-to-end journeys. One Native run saved all eight replies and then received four stream 503s on return. The actual caller reproduces both contention points with a recoverable protected save held: transport registration and the initial view's read boundary. A real queued quit never registers, and revoked scope returns 403. Both permitted streams receive their initial resets after the held turn settles. Both graceful shutdown and the injected cold failure pass, and a fresh server resumes at the durable cut. Its steady figures describe observed cost and do not close adoption.

The existing A/B/C, encoder, disclosure/admission, corrected receiving/departure and selected player/NPC/PostgreSQL pipeline retain their component evidence. The direct-header and naturally aged clock diagnostic is complete; its measured CPU subset does not establish the exclusive full-tick target.

The [isolated empty place comparison](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-17-native-active-area-comparisons.md#empty-place-read-on-the-first-walk--november-17-2026) verifies three pairs per runtime and retains the exact-cut query removal. ### Applicable multiplayer timing policy

Ordinary player commands return their complete live in-memory result before PostgreSQL durability. Apply the existing 100/200 ms p95/p99 interaction target to that command response and 200/350 ms to first visible movement. Name command arrival, acceptance, HTTP response, first affected view and durable commit ACK separately. Ordinary storage targets one real second from the first unsaved acceptance, retains five-second/byte backpressure and the existing last-complete-cut crash-loss promise. Measure actual save age and command-to-ACK against that policy; do not introduce synchronous durability or assign a 200 ms ACK promise to the one-second scheduler.

The prior combined journey's forced-flush latency numbers remain valid for that fixture. Its first command waits for another command, eight game steps and intervening view requests before the explicit flush. Those values do not establish ordinary live-response or normal background-save tails. Recovery replacement and its known full reinsertion remain separate from steady gameplay database costs. The prior complete-walk count (Source 23/Native 10, same 69 prepared records/42,681 bytes) remains measured evidence; actual redundant queries/captures in the steady window require attribution before changing their owners.

## Delivery stages

**Current disposition:** the selected NSC01–NSC03 proof/evaluation is complete at 2 GiB, with Node-hosted Rust adoption rejected by its E1 comparison. The scoped E2 investigation and individual E3 dispositions are complete. NSC04–NSC08 retain their engineering dependency order. The stand-alone full-game design is complete; supported conversion and cell/distributed/adaptive delivery are unfinished. Historical 64/128 MiB assessments retain their recorded scope. Missing correctness or capacity is never called delivered.

- [x] **NSC00 — Architecture and staged decision design.** Specify compact resident authority, full smaller pipeline, host/native boundaries, exact personal evidence and restore, work pooling, cells, machine handoff, adaptive placement, isolation experiments and removal targets. Source inspection, primary research and affected documentation review establish design completion only. The [design pair](../projects/native-simulation-core-feature-spec.md) and E1–E7 are the deliverables.

- [x] **NSC01 — Comparison contract and minimal native host.** After implementation authorization, pin the latest integrated source and selected family/input inventory; reconcile BK29 and all relevant existing acceptance. Establish the [caller/field coverage manifest](../projects/native-simulation-core-tech-design.md#94-full-adoption-inventory-and-removal-targets) before converting each owner, including indirect readers and finalization duties. Build the two-crate workspace, exact toolchain/lockfile, generated boundary types, bounded native lifecycle and current-format installation. Prepare a narrow equivalent compact-state TS control for the replaced hot path, not a second full engine. **Exit:** explicit supported/refused installation, one authority per proof world, ready/accept/reject lifecycle with host-current input and capacity fences, bounded cancellation/rollback and shared operation checkpoints, generated NAPI declarations with real runtime conversion, no raw world crossing, per-field FFI or hidden live TS fallback. Pin comparison criteria before runs. Correctness/setup only does not establish performance.

- [x] **NSC02 — Complete one-worker gameplay proof.** Depends on NSC01. Port the entire declared movement/action/rate → sensing/local identity → attention/history → player/NPC capture → actual PostgreSQL save/cold-restore pipeline. Include real native animal reaction/continuation, speech, independent stale detail, unknown identity, correction/forgetting and pending-result fences. Preserve current numerical policies and the same semantic work in both arms. **Exit:** all declared [proof journeys](../projects/native-simulation-core-tech-design.md#91-freeze-the-comparison-contract) and applicable conversion cases pass through real consumers, including complete context/reference/trigger equivalence; no empty save/recording stubs. Rejection restores state/RNG/history without debt subtraction; partial refusal and previews preserve their established outcomes. Exercise private-candidate read isolation, capacity refusal before acceptance, partial/cold history and unsaved query overlays, semantic rather than global invalidation, protected pre-effect facts, failed saves and current privacy/identity fences on restore. Run E1/E3 only after the implemented stage is complete. **The selected caller/field closure, current 2 GiB complete gameplay and required continuation/failure evidence are complete at the recorded identities. This does not pass the performance/adoption gate or complete the supported-game families in NSC04.**

- [x] **NSC03 — Bounded parallel proof and adoption decision.** Depends on complete NSC02. Add one shared native compute pool, existing phase barriers, typed disjoint ranges and deterministic effects/RNG; no per-interval generic dependency graph or repeated shared input copies. Compare identical one/multiple-worker execution with bridge, Node consumers, database, allocation and waiting included. Exercise tiny, crowded and independent workloads. **Exit:** E1–E3 report faithful complete-path costs and isolated variant results against preselected evaluation targets; a narrow TS control does not isolate the whole pipeline's language benefit. Record **adopt**, **reject** or **inconclusive**. A reject/inconclusive result records that candidate/profile; repair or remove the specific unsuccessful mechanism and continue other authorized implementation. Numerical targets remain evaluation criteria, not project-stop conditions. A fast isolated kernel cannot pass this gate. **Current scoped assessment: Reject the measured Native candidate at 2 GiB. The final matched E1 completes all six runs; all eighteen repaired width runs complete and reject the changed-record split. The whole-phase E2 investigation closes with its measured negative eligibility result. Keep same-world dispatch and inline encoding. Full parallel simulation and broader conversion remain undelivered. Historical 64/128 MiB results retain their identities.**

**Stand-alone handoff.** The [RGS tracker](completed/2026/rust-game-server.md) records the selected opening and scoped decision. The [caller inventory](native-simulation-core-coverage.md) tracks which NSC04 families were exercised and which still need work. Use the frozen one-worker baseline in the [multicore continuation](#within-world-multicore-continuation). Remaining Node bridge work stays with [NRF](native-runtime-foundations.md#work-and-exit-criteria). NSC05–NSC08 retain the dependencies and acceptance gates below.

- [ ] **NSC04 — Complete supported-game conversion.** After coherent preceding semantics/interfaces, complete and reconcile NSC01's caller/field/command coverage manifest against the latest integrated source. Port the installed world-family closure and convert action/menu/inspection, body/status/inventory/assembly, authoring, grant, native response, cognition, memory and current-format storage callers together. Retire replaced Immer/draft/certification/diff and dual-authority paths after assigning every meaningful finalization duty. **Exit:** real standard opening and ordinary browser/NPC/save/restore journeys pass; no supported field, operation or indirect reader is unmapped, silently defaulted or served by a hidden TS world. Future unimplemented feature families retain their own trackers. Preserve useful starting content; no legacy reader or permanent backend switch.

- [ ] **NSC05 — Same-machine cells and interaction ownership.** Depends on NSC04 for full-game adoption; a scoped E4 can run earlier on the proof without claiming full delivery. Separate geometry membership from cell ownership and core scheduling. Implement conservative read overlap, exact conflict ownership and border-stable routing; no network stack is required. **Exit:** equal interior/edge/corner outcomes, large bodies/attachments, converging/dispersing groups, real heard/received history and scoped privacy; no duplicated simulation per player, full-cell copy per tick or unconditional global scan. Include E4's added metadata/copy costs.

- [ ] **NSC06 — Controlled distributed execution and custody.** Depends on correct NSC05 ownership/influence boundaries; measured capacity and coordination cost guide the implementation. Coordinate with D6/PD12; retain one existing writer, same-operator/same-build world and exact affected-scope timing. Implement declared influence scopes, bounded prepared exchanges and prepare/quiesce/stage/fence/activate handoff. Preserve current provider jobs across routing changes. Use coherent world cuts and whole-world failure recovery initially; independently advancing scopes and live precopy are separately measured E5 variants. Keep unsupported/global scopes colocated. **Exit:** E5 establishes useful capacity after network/synchronization costs; commands, prepared responses and save batches occur once through failure before/after fencing, restart, stale replies and cross-boundary action/receipt/save paths; transfer pauses and stalled global cuts are included. No global per-action RPC barrier, speculative rollback, full past-memory transfer or independently sharded storage. No production approval follows from a two-machine experiment.

- [ ] **NSC07 — Adaptive grouping and hysteresis.** Depends on NSC06; prototype placement decisions against captured counters without executing migration first. Add bounded actual-work/communication counters, neighboring-cell candidates, destination capacity checks and amortized transfer decisions. Reuse existing boundary data and immutable resources; retain no full former-owner replica. **Exit:** isolated E6 beats fixed assignments on complete stationary/moving/cycling/dispersal workloads without churn, enlarged overlap debt, gameplay barriers or starvation. If adaptive work costs more, remove it and retain static assignments. Exact operating values remain measured tuning, not world rules.

- [ ] **NSC08 — Adoption cleanup and existing qualification integration.** At each adopted boundary, update current contracts, manifests, limits, removal evidence and the scope of completed tasks. Remove rejected experiments and replace unsupported performance claims with source/workload-specific results. Coordinate dispersed-player, crowded-scene, boundary, mature-memory and failure acceptance through PF/PS/PD rather than a new suite. **Exit:** applicable stage obligations pass or are explicitly failed; no unfinished agreed conversion is relabeled a future improvement. Final production capacity and enjoyment remain their actual evidence owners.

Start R1 with one real reply. R2 can proceed with its own typed maintenance request and protected save while other handoffs are converted. R3 can start on the supported opening while R4 adds the positive inventory and action families needed by Main. Join each R5 caller as soon as its behavior is ready. R6 is the release gate.

### Within-world multicore continuation

**Stand-alone performance batch — November 19, 2026.** The reviewed `de0d467cd` release is the new comparison control. Trace real browser walks on the managed 16 GiB worker, joining the command ID and accepted cut to the first changed stream position and displayed movement. Record ordinary save capture, confirmed commit and acknowledgement separately. Compare typical and slow completed moves with the operation holding each delayed resource. Independently attribute PostgreSQL reader waits, size and body selects, write preparation and transaction work. Compare visibility admission and retained bytes on one fixed route with valid distant scenery added. Select a multicore family only after the real batch and phase evidence supports useful independent work. Use the same repaired source and pinned workload within each comparison, three interleaved repeats per arm, fresh databases, 2 GiB, ordinary saves and controlled HTTPS. The existing [timing policy](#applicable-multiplayer-timing-policy), [E2 gates](#first-proof-qualification-criteria) and [managed-worker workflow](../../.agents/rules/verification-workers.md) own the decision and cleanup. RGS08 remains historical evidence.

The first three managed diagnostics are collected and settled. The PostgreSQL trace completed. The attached browser trace expired before gameplay, and the first fixed-route visibility comparison failed its Cargo lock check before gameplay. The [managed attribution record](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-18-rust-game-server.md#managed-attribution) keeps those outcomes and the identified successors. Initial investigation found that active sight uses rays and convex visibility certificates. The sweep bound has no live simulation caller and cannot establish retained memory or multicore eligibility for walking.

The [stand-alone continuation evidence](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-18-rust-game-server.md#stand-alone-performance-continuation--november-19-2026) closes the receipt-read stall. Separate simulation and operational connections let the clock and ordinary save proceed while the exact receipt read remains held. All six clean managed runs qualified. The combined CPU difference was inside the run spread. The fixed-route visibility diagnostic retained equal sight answers with 192 additional distant physical pieces. The repair for an underfunded convex answer passed the production detour, ordinary save and a real capacity refusal followed by successful continuation.

The completed browser trace joins nine walks from rest through the final authorized view to displayed motion. On the slowest two walks, final disclosure waited roughly 96–108 ms, mostly behind the elapsed clock's world turn. Final authorization and encoding took less than 0.45 ms once the turn arrived. The six-run clean comparison supports retaining the 50 ms window measured from preparation start. Typical first-movement receipt improved by 17.2%, with an uneven p95 result and more PostgreSQL read activity. The one-second background save and its separate acknowledgement window remain in use.

No runtime change or multicore qualification follows from this diagnostic.

**Perception cost follow-up — November 20, 2026.** The first comparison bundles four changes that preserve gameplay. Look up due detail subjects directly, read attention deadlines without rebuilding the ordered anchor set, omit unused history directories from stand-alone history preparation, and skip encoding unchanged plain attention latches and scans with the same stored position. Local checks covered identity, order and visibility, both replies, required private history and cold continuation. All six managed runs on the heavy 26-actor opening completed the required work. Keep the bundle with 14.5% lower median combined CPU per completed game-second and lower p95 movement receipt. The trial does not isolate each change.

The independent authored trial limits spare ordinary body-tracking cues for human NPCs and preserves broad perception, speech, self warnings, directed and required work, and player detail. All six paid bystander runs completed. The Base limit is now installed, with 14.6% lower combined median CPU per completed game-second, unchanged achieved speed and lower p95 movement receipt. Median movement receipt was 19 ms slower. Keep the separate change that serves preferences and AI display from one read transaction. It eliminated one transaction per state response in the fixed-state caller check, and median total commits fell 24.3% in the completed six-run comparison. Complete CPU was inconclusive. Local integrated checks passed cold continuation, private forgetting and durable replies. The [comparison record](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-20-perception-cost-experiments.md) owns frozen inputs, evidence and follow-up opportunities.

- [x] **NSC04-H1 — Observation reads.** Keep the narrower warning scan. - [x] **NSC04-H4 — Save and decision inputs.** The established save path remains after the equal-row, one-pass and sparse-fold comparisons. The narrower original NPC read reduced peak memory. Selected-source withdrawal, unrelated activity, relative movement from the original frame, held-save custody and exact cold receipts passed. [Steps and checks](../projects/completed/2026/native-simulation-hot-paths.md#4-prepare-fewer-storage-and-decision-products)

Directed conversation proceeded during the wait, and movement that removed the only cue prevented the optional job. A quieter deer policy still requires attribution of nearby witness work to deer movement. The optional change to bystander movement memory in profiler proposal 2 remains a product decision. These experiments preserve the existing authored behavior, and leave the separate multicore gates in place.

### NPC and background-work follow-up

The [profiling findings](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-20-rust-hot-path-comparisons.md#next-profiling-candidates-outside-movement-perception-and-saving) define this batch. The [evidence record](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-20-rust-npc-background-work.md) records the collected comparisons, real caller checks and fixture failures. The [final joint build](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-20-rust-npc-background-work.md#final-ordinary-play) completed the ordinary opening, both saved conversations and cold continuation on the standard worker.

| Experiment          | Implementation and completion check                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| NPC memory review   | Retained narrow recall preparation. Combined CPU per completed game-second fell 3.8–7.2% in the three frozen pairs. The actual decision still prepares fresh executable choices.                                                                           |
| Resident scheduling | The frozen one-intake version used 2.9–6.8% less combined CPU per completed game-second and ran fewer automatic judgments in every pair. The exact-text reader supersedes it. The separate narrow speech lookup was removed after the ordinary comparison. |
| Job-status display  | Retained the indexed compact projection of the same newest 12 jobs. Both arms preserved permission, ordering and exact text. Scan work fell in every pair. Complete CPU was inconclusive. The current format requires a fresh development database.        |
| Body deadlines      | Retained the shared attribute and threshold reading after exact sleep, starvation and saved continuation checks. Combined CPU varied, with no repeatable speed improvement. The Base definitions give an installed-rule index nothing to skip.             |
| Search indexing     | Reuse of identical text passed the real indexing and forgetting checks. The ordinary comparison did not justify keeping the added lookup and ownership.                                                                                                    |

The stand-alone scheduler can defer a temporary intake allocation refusal or a confirmed rollback to its next world update. Job status and explicit retry share the same exact stored authority. Recovery clears former-timeline speech links within the checkpoint transaction while preserving the other job values. Both servers preserve exact admitted speech while preparing SQL-safe reference and search fields. The [evidence record](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-20-rust-npc-background-work.md#november-21-review-checks) distinguishes these correctness repairs from the frozen performance experiments.

The stand-alone scheduler uses the save loader's number rules for observation sequences. The reply formatter decodes the fields it uses and leaves history and validity with the native read that authorizes dispatch. This includes [pending intentions](../action-experience.md#storage-and-extension-boundary), whose saved text stays exact. The shared packet encoder carries byte refusal through to the saved conversation outcome, allowing explicit retry. The [real gameplay checks](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-20-rust-npc-background-work.md#pending-intentions-and-packet-admission) cover permitted text, privacy, withdrawal, cancellation, packet refusal, retry and recovery. Revisit further native capture reductions when profiling shows a material cost.

The matched comparisons used the same 26-actor stand-alone opening, ordinary clock and background saves, two controlled saved conversations, and cold continuation. Three interleaved repetitions on standard workers used independent databases. The single final joint run checked the retained implementation through the same gameplay and an additional mixed-ownership status case.

The [isolated reply-packet comparison](../benchmarks/native-runtime-foundations/2026/2026-11-20-rust-npc-background-work.md#full-versus-smaller-reply-packet) retains the smaller packet with lower transient reservations in all three pairs. Complete CPU savings remain inconclusive because the runs did different amounts of background NPC work.

## Overlap and dependencies

| Existing owner                                            | Reconciliation                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| --------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| PF10 — Long-lived simulation worker                       | NSC01–NSC05 supplies a concrete selected design for the resident-worker path. Existing path/serialization workers stay delivered; browser prediction is separate.                                                                     |
| BK29-A–D                                                  | Retention, attention preparation and utility work remain current-runtime tasks. Reuse accepted semantics and evidence; do not require a speculative native rewrite to unblock them. A retired mechanism is not a retired requirement. |
| BK29-E                                                    | One owner for outward activity/escape-leg and notification semantics. Apply its accepted result equally to comparison arms; no second event framework in Rust.                                                                        |
| PP08 / RU / SR / PS04-A                                   | Shared reception, current detail, identities, chronology, minimal support and consumers are preservation requirements, not features to re-invent or mark newly delivered.                                                             |
| D22 / D61 / R06 / R12 / R13                               | Same-world continuous geography, cells and custody have a designed target. Actual density, coupled-family distribution, failure cost and broader federation remain unqualified; no unlimited-capacity decision.                       |
| D6 / PD11–PD12                                            | Native compute conversion precedes independent storage distribution. Multiple worlds may be scheduled separately without proving same-world regional scaling.                                                                         |
| Existing AI, physical sensing and first-playable trackers | No new model loop, vision/acoustic production feature, behavior retuning or content removal. Their delivery/quality requirements remain unchanged.                                                                                    |

## Verification boundaries

Use the [technical experiment ledger](../projects/native-simulation-core-tech-design.md#10-experiments-and-adoption-gates) and current [verification policy](../../.agents/rules/verification.md). Finish each NSC experiment before its isolated AWS comparison. The separate [RGS comparison plan](../projects/completed/2026/rust-game-server-tech-design.md#8-compare-and-decide-rgs08) measures the integrated server and selects extra trials by the decision they resolve. No unit-suite run or new maintained test suite is selected. Correctness may reuse focused checks and disposable actual-consumer scenarios; all speed testing uses the managed standard 16 GiB AWS worker unless Mike explicitly approves another environment. Required numerical gates remain with the dedicated qualification owner and must be fixed before measurement.

### First-proof qualification criteria

Use the same H0 instance, software, authored definitions, controlled responses and elapsed/input schedule for each paired comparison. Use at least three runs of each arm, with interleaved order and independent databases. Record the exact build, clock, accepted work, output and saved rows in each run. A semantic mismatch, omitted required work, growing debt or an exceeded ownership limit fails the affected comparison. A failure in setup or collection has no measured gameplay result.

Use the existing [small-world performance targets](performance.md#acceptance-budgets) for command and confirmed-movement tails, view emission, 50 ms tick work and the ordinary walk SQL round-trip ceiling. Measure Node process CPU, including its native and JavaScript workers, and exclusive PostgreSQL CPU over the same completed work. Retain the whole-run latency distributions, physical memory and native reservations, and SQL calls, rows, bytes, WAL and elapsed work. Keep diagnostic samples separate from clean runs.

For E1, a material CPU improvement is at least 10% lower median combined CPU per completed workload, with each pair improving and the improvement exceeding observed repeat variation. Required latency and resource limits also apply. For E2, target a throughput gain or completion p95 reduction of at least 10% on a crowded or dispersed workload, with at most 5% additional combined CPU per work unit and no measured tiny-scene regression beyond repeat variation. For E3, keep the encoding variant only when the same rules and provider complete the same work and the measured whole path earns a material benefit or removes a binding memory limit. A smaller or variable effect is inconclusive. These project-specific targets are selected before gameplay timing and do not establish production limits. Under the November 13 continuation they guide candidate repair/retention, not whether remaining designed stages are implemented. Correct simpler neutral code can be retained with a stated justification; complexity and material regressions still require evidence.

The disposable compact TypeScript control compares the selected physical access, placement write, scoped change and capture with the current Source draft/transition. It preserves exact support, optional continuity, heading, revision, visible and tentative values and all six final body rows over 96 inputs, including nine candidate rejections. The untimed comparison passes on Node 22.23.2 with digest `a10a92511f761b94bd3976e77f782c8b21fba283ffb3dffb907ade8b69a05880` in `/tmp/openlegend-nsc-compact-position-correctness2.log`. It isolates those representation operations. The full Source/native comparison includes the rest of the gameplay and storage work.

The first managed H0 readiness runs use exact source `db20d31630a21147ed5bf3b456c930a7bf539337` on the same `m7i.xlarge` in `us-east-2a`, with two physical cores, four logical CPUs and an exclusive PostgreSQL 16.15 cluster with pgvector. Campaign `7e758702-3102-4796-8014-57271d643686` verifies current workspace and database bindings. Campaign `368df4f2-b81a-47c3-8018-fef448a035e5` uses checksummed case-local Node 22.23.2, Rust 1.99.1, Zig 0.15.2 and the locked vendor cache. The actual x86-64 release addon with source-line tables builds and repeats the generated interface. Both runs cleanly release the worker. Its existing unprivileged case policy refuses even a user-space `perf task-clock` on the case's own child at `perf_event_paranoid=4`. Native sampled attribution remains open. Neither run measures gameplay.

The joined Linux release correctness comparison passes on the same standard H0 worker in campaign `4baa6875-c842-45c9-8735-756e640c2ace`, run `16dd3533162e2d79f03693200241805d`, generation 221. It builds exact commit `6b4d74983d4b54d4535c4548ec17bfc981b635b6` and addon SHA-256 `70c1b9aa2c44d7f5c9fac83cd89d21d28839b2a5fbce6ad7739ba8a2dbfef092` with Node 22.23.2 and the locked Rust 1.99.1 toolchain. The worker qualifies 697 x64 angle inputs against the same Node binary. Its two independent owned databases use the witnessed exclusive PostgreSQL 16.15 cluster. The real requests complete 138 elapsed steps, 24 walks and two NPC conversations in each arm, then save, reopen, restore the checkpoint and continue. All four cuts match 166 canonical tables and command receipts under the existing raw encoding and actual collector-prefix rules. Native reaches 47,863,375 transient reserved bytes and closes with every reservation zero. The runner verifies cleanup and releases the retained worker to idle. Timing is off. The result does not establish CPU, RSS, latency or an adoption outcome.

The panic recovery case passes `/tmp/openlegend-nsc-panic-current2.log`. A disposable addon adds one fault to the real bridge after the armed movement candidate's provider completions and before Ready. The application rejects subsequent native reads and public output, and all 166 SQL tables stay identical to the last accepted cut. The poisoned writer cannot finish a normal stop, so recovery opens that exact durable cut with the unmodified addon in a separate process. Actual input resumes the accepted walk, saves through simulation second 1 and closes with zero reservations. The case uses Node source `250e1c0e20f9e70e0f3d467c3bbaca5a8b43939506985ee1929e1100bfe5ce4e`, diagnostic native `a0525afdb11b6b057ca3300f0cea7951d52c3ae91caf21805a02d842ebdff637` and unchanged native `8d74778021bc6e0655a8ce14d4563eadc5f0f145b4baa0d3845a96097b5ece47`. The first fault location missed provider-resumed work. The accepted case arms the real movement call and observes the same candidate at the prepared bridge boundary. No maintained fault API was added.

**Design-task evidence:** affected source/contract reading, public primary-source research, feature/technical/limits reconciliation, local link/anchor checking and changed-file formatting. No gameplay, profiling, cloud or paid model execution is claimed.

**Open product questions:** None for the first proof. Candidate adoption and later experimental enablement retain correctness and engineering dependencies; missed percentage targets do not stop the authorized implementation. This tracker does not resolve unrelated federation, unfamiliar world-law support or product congestion decisions by assumption.
